Will brings a struggle over power with it, because what sustains a life can also threaten it, and a person seeks ways to ward off, enlist or command those powers; the desire to control is present long before the rational techniques that later give it other means.

In the mythical structure those powers become distinguishable through their relations and transformations, and the means of knowing is the told story. Oral history carries a people's experience across generations by compressing it: centuries of encounter with storm, harvest, death, desire and betrayal condense into a few figures and their deeds, so that a myth is psychodynamics compressed into a form that can be remembered and retold. Raw force, which magical consciousness met through sensation, becomes a god with a name, a temper and a history, and the encounter with it becomes a relation one can feel one's way into. Feeling leads here, since the mythical world is known through what things are worth, whom they bind and whom they wound. Day and night, life and death, mother and father are polarities within a shared reality, each receiving its character through the other, and seasonal rites give time a rhythm in which loss and renewal are understood together. Myth is the first home of the mytheme, the image that proves a relation through the action it stages. Modern habit treats myth as an invented explanation laid over a self-evident material world, and so reverses the situation. For mythical consciousness the gods are powers through which the world is met at all; they give desire, conflict and transformation an experienced form long before anyone classifies them as psychological contents. A telling turns restrictive when every new encounter must repeat an inherited role, and that restriction depends on myth's real capacity to make a life intelligible.

In the mental structure those inherited forms become available to explicit examination, and the story becomes an episteme, a reasoned science of the world and of Mind. A speaker distinguishes a claim from its bearer, asks for reasons, follows consequences and compares an explanation with what it purports to explain; a god can become the subject of a proposition whose truth is questioned, and the proposition no longer borrows authority from the divine name merely because the name occurs in it. With thinking leading, a person gains a definite position from which to judge, and the world is set before that position: painting's three-dimensional, perspectival achievement made into a way of knowing. Objects can be located in a common space, their relations calculated and their behaviour tested by procedures others can repeat, and this makes possible the sustained criticism of appearances that ran from Russell and Whitehead to Gödel. It also tempts mental consciousness to take reality for whatever its own criticism can admit. Images are then treated as ornaments, magic as error and spiritual recognition as a belief awaiting a better explanation. Mental consciousness turns deficient when its power to distinguish hardens into the authority to disqualify every other way of knowing, including those through which its own purposes and discoveries arise: it takes credit for a truth it holds in common. The previous movement has already shown that deficient phase arising from rationality's own products. Russell's paradox, Gödel's sentence and the hard problem were each reached by reasoning at its most exact, and each brought reason to a threshold it had produced for itself, a point where its own procedures returned what they could not render rational. The deficiency shows in how that threshold is met. Gebser insists on the difference between the irrational and the arational: the irrational falls below measure and disregards it, while the arational knows and respects the measurable and is free of it, and it is impossible without the pre-rational, the irrational and the rational, which together form its basis.145 Deficient mental consciousness can meet its threshold only as the irrational, something to exclude, fear or explain away, which is why Russell sought certainty against paradox and why a culture defers its blind spot to a future physics. Met as the arational, the same threshold is where the integral begins: the contradiction becomes, in Whitehead's words, "the first step in progress towards a victory", and the limit becomes the basis of faith as well as of investigation.146

Wolfgang Giegerich's The End of Meaning and the Birth of Man describes the severity of this historical shift as a birth out of the encompassing world of inherited meaning.147 What once contained the person becomes something the person can set before themselves and evaluate. A myth deliberately chosen to restore that containment already bears the mark of the freedom from it that made choosing possible, so modern consciousness cannot simply decide to recover the innocence of being held by an unquestioned world. Freedom from the old forms leaves our relation to the powers they expressed unsettled. Many gods can be subordinated to one God, their conflicts interpreted as events within the psyche, and divine sovereignty finally rejected in favour of a self-sufficient material universe, while desire, terror, devotion and the need to belong go on organising life through every shift. A society may cease to recognise the sacred character of what it serves while investing nation, market or technical progress with demands once made in the name of God: its account turns secular while its submission remains absolute. We face a post-symbolic exposure. We are responsible for understanding powers that the inherited images no longer authoritatively explain, and that rational description alone has not brought under conscious command.

The integral structure requires these ways of knowing to become present together in their actual operation, and Gebser called that co-presence diaphaneity, a showing-through. Things keep their particularity while their appearance discloses the relations through which we meet them: a mathematical description is understood together with its assumptions, the intuition that prompted it and the purposes directing its use; a sacred image is recognised both in what it reveals and in how it organises a person's hopes and fears. The integral stands at #4, the position QL names Context, and it works by contextualising. Magic's efficacious sign, myth's proving image and the mental's warranted account, the first homes of matheme, mytheme and episteme, each claimed to be the whole of knowing because each forgot the circumstances in which it knows. Diaphaneity lets those circumstances show through, so that each way of knowing appears within the context of its use, and that sight voids its claim to stand outside every context. Their difference stays and their rivalry for sole possession of truth ends. The voiding is the first act in recognising the symbolon, since pieces that had each mistaken themselves for the whole are found to fit. Intuition leads in this structure because it can hold the fit: it is the unity of thinking and feeling, the capacity to grasp a whole situation with judgment and with care at once.

What the integral recognises in Logos is a recovery of what the word always carried. Logos comes from legein, whose earlier senses are to gather, to pick up and lay together, and then to count and recount, before it settles into to speak; Heidegger read Heraclitus' Logos as this laying that gathers.148 Jung's pairing of Logos as discrimination, judgment and insight with Eros as the placing into relation describes two motions the root already held together, and Russell's desire for certainty was that eros already at work in a logician. A Logos that only cuts has forgotten its own gathering, and the integral remembers it, so the symbolon it recognises is the symbolon of the eros of Logos. Gebser's aperspectival follows from this recovery: perspective loses its exclusive authority, and we can occupy a position clearly while recognising the other structures through which it has become meaningful. His arational names a reasoning that no longer requires reality to end at reason's present boundary.

Aurobindo's Supermind names the affirmative shift that follows the voiding at #4: where the integral sees each structure in its context, in the supramental a person identifies as the totality of the mutations, the archaic participant, the magician, the teller of myths and the reasoner known as the layers of one life and owned without any one of them presiding, as an elder at best owns every age they have lived. That identification never settles into a possession: it is a constant passage between #4 and #5, Context and Quintessence, in which every new context is received into the whole and the whole is tested again in every context. 5→0 names the further turn, the spiritually realised state in which one who identifies as the totality of the mutations identifies also with and as the Origin, what always is and what is becoming through all of them. Our opening question, "What knows?", now has a clearer answer: Origin knows, through every structure in which it becomes manifest, and the person asking knows as Origin as far as that identification reaches; a world gathered into an account includes the gathering through which it becomes this account.

The structures do not follow one another like slides in a projector, and two thinkers name the two dimensions of the thickness of phenomenal experience that explain why. Henri Bergson began Time and Free Will (1889) against the habit of measuring lived time as if it were space, in which the intellect sets moments side by side like points on a line and arrives at a homogeneous, countable time that nobody has ever lived. What we live he called durée, duration: the continuous qualitative life in which what has just occurred permeates what is occurring now, so that successive states melt into one another and form a whole without ever becoming a row.149 Listening to a tune, we hear each sounding note through the phrase already underway, and the note can fulfil an expectation, prolong a tension or change how the earlier notes are heard; set out note by note, the same tune turns into a picture of a melody, the spatialised time Bergson diagnosed. He argued in the same book that a free act springs from the whole self living in duration, and he later made intuition the method by which philosophy enters duration from within, which brings his thought close to the function that leads the integral.150 Turn within the painter's room and one wall passes out of view while another appears, yet we go on inhabiting one room; what has disappeared stays implicated in our orientation, and returning to the first view does not restore the first experience unchanged, because we have lived through the turn. Edmund Husserl named the other dimension sedimentation: what a culture once grasped with full insight settles into its language, habits and instruments, where later generations inherit it as obvious, usable and largely unexamined, until someone reactivates the act that first gave it sense.151 Duration thickens experience across moments; sedimentation thickens it across generations. Gebser's mutations stack in exactly this way, as layers laid down in the collective mind and, within each life, in the personal mind, laminated so that a magical reflex, a mythic loyalty and a mental argument can all act in a single gesture. Bratton reaches for the same image when he describes how an artificial agent is composed: "not a monad but a lamination", in which the persona is one stratum among many.152 Bergson's duration folds through these laminae: the past is active within present understanding and never deposited outside it as a finished object, so a later discovery can alter what an earlier event meant. In this layered thickness Objective Internality becomes palpable, as the relation in which objects become subjective forms and the Subject knows through them. Gebser's time-freedom is release from the exclusive rule of any one temporal form: magical participation, mythical rhythm and mental succession become consciously available within the present without any one being declared real at the expense of the others, and the ever-present Origin becomes recognisable through the whole differentiated life, the history of its recognition included.

Gebser's dimensional progression follows increasingly adequate ways of articulating a reality that includes the act of articulation. Perspective coordinates appearances from a position; integral knowing includes the lived transformations through which positions become available to one another, and §3 constructs these relations mathematically, through rotation, projection and double covering. When the QL positions are assigned, they will have to express one activity, Mind gathering a world through forms of knowing that are themselves present within that world. Each of those forms extends credit, issues claims, keeps accounts and assigns value, and the words for these operations belong to economy, whose name comes from the household.

Greek oikonomia, from which we get economy, meant the management of an oikos, a household: the people, provisions, property and work through which a shared life was maintained. Its nom- belongs to nemein, to distribute, allot or pasture, and nomos, custom and law, began as an allotted grazing before it became the ordering through which distributions are established; the nomad still moves between such allotments, and anomie names a life left without its share. A younger word for the same house is just as exact: Ernst Haeckel coined Oecologie in 1866 from oikos joined to logos, so that economy and ecology name the two gestures of one household: nemein distributes it, and legein gathers it into an account of what keeps it alive.153 A mind distributes attention and gathers what it has attended to into a world; a life distributes energy among its organs and gathers them into one metabolism; a world distributes places among its beings and gathers them into a common habitation. An economy that has lost its logos goes on allotting a house it no longer accounts for, and the ecological crisis is that loss written into the earth. These words take us into a world where knowing how to live already involves deciding what belongs to whom, who may act, and what each owes to the others. Aristotle's household included relations between parent and child, husband and wife, master and enslaved person; its unity was organised through unequal powers, and the one who administered it did not necessarily recognise everyone sustaining it as equally entitled to determine its purposes. His distinction between household provision and unlimited acquisition, chrēmatistikē, still holds for the same reason: obtaining more is a different undertaking from providing what a life together requires, and an economy can become highly effective at the first while damaging the relations that make the second possible. He kept his sharpest judgment for usury, because Greek called interest tokos, offspring, and money bred from money seemed to him the most unnatural of births.154 A household is also a family, its generations overlapping, each inheriting a share it did not earn and owing a care it did not choose. Economics and law, number and word, seem to be the primary determinants of life as it is lived, personal and familial before they become national or global.

Value and rule meet explicitly in nomisma, coin or current money. In the Nicomachean Ethics Aristotle connected the word with nomos: money lets unlike goods and different kinds of work be compared through an accepted measure whose value depends on convention.154 A coin is itself a 0/1: the blank disc is its ground, the die strikes a determination into it, and current value is their relation. Greek called the die-stamp charaktēr and the impression typos, from which an archetype, a first impression, still takes its name; around the face runs the legend, legenda, what is to be read, and the denomination gives the measure a name and a count. A stamped token circulates because people recognise what may be claimed through it, though the token itself neither produces the provisions nor performs the labour for which it is exchanged. Token grows from the root *deik-, to show or point, which also gives index and digit, and a token points past itself to a trust already extended, so that its value is a presence held on loan. Currency runs, from currere, and what is current is whatever is present enough to pass from hand to hand; money borrows that presence from the work, goods and lives it points to, and value, from valere, to be strong or well, is a strength it keeps only while the loan is honoured. We approach an economy of mind through this relation between a sign, its accepted value and the order in which it becomes effective. A divine name, an ancestral title, a legal category or a numerical score directs attention and conduct because people have learned what it authorises. Through legein, gathering and saying, they bring experience into a communicable account; through their nomos, they establish which accounts may govern what happens next. Knowing and ruling are already distinct, and already entangled, within the use of language itself: calling someone a child, a citizen, a subject or a resource assigns them a place from which quite different claims may be heard. English keeps that entanglement in its oldest words of exchange, since owe and own grow from one root, āgan, to have, a debt is de-habere, something had from another, and interest, inter-esse, is what lies between. Interest charged on a loan prices the between itself, the very slash through which two parties are related, and counts it to one side: the appropriation given a rate, the lender's (+1)−(−1)=+2 being the borrower's (−1)−(+1)=−2.155

An oikonomia therefore concerns how a whole sustains its differentiated members and what becomes of the powers apportioned among them, well beyond an inventory of possessions. Ephesians extends the word to the divine ordering of the fullness of times, oikonomia tou plērōmatos, in which what belongs to heaven and earth is gathered together in Christ, so that the administration of a household becomes a language for manifestation, history and their fulfilment.156 The Church Fathers went on to distinguish within the divine life itself between theologia, God as God is, and oikonomia, God's dispensation in creation and history (Tertullian's dispensatio, a weighing-out), and Western theology has laboured ever since to keep the two from coming apart, until Karl Rahner restated their unity in his rule that the economic Trinity is the immanent Trinity.157 Read through our notation, the divine economy is syn-ballein, (0/1)/(1/0): the One dispensed into the Many and the Many gathered back, with neither half becoming the owner of the whole. Kashmiri Śaivism holds the relation as a co-equal pair. Śiva names the Subject in its speakable aspect, and Śakti, his consort, is the power of that Subject to differentiate and take on identities both as means of knowing and as objects of knowledge: Śiva is Mono, Śakti is Poly. Such metaphysical dynamics cannot remain abstract when they account for the same dynamics that decide whether or not I get to eat tomorrow, or have a house to hold me at all. Every finite capacity operates within a reality it does not privately originate: whoever knows receives language, whoever commands receives an office, and whoever makes something receives materials and powers of making. Independence is a particular achievement within dependence. Sovereignty turns into a problem when an allotted capacity is used to claim possession of the whole it derives from, and the inversion lies already in the ground of our own terms. Our Poly is polys, many; the poly of monopoly is pōlein, to sell, so that in a monopoly the One stands where the many should be and faces, in their place, only what is sold. Aristotle records the first instance in the very chapter where he separates provision from acquisition: Thales, foreseeing a large olive harvest, hired every press in Miletus and Chios and let them out at whatever price he chose.158 The diabolical economy is dia-ballein, its own namesake. Its polar form, (−1)/(+1), holds two parties in tension across a relation both depend on; its collapsed form, (−1)±(+1), either cancels them into 0 or lets one of them count the whole span as its own, +2 or −2. The 1 then takes its own measure as the measure of reality, and those who cannot live under it are required to explain their failure.

The Dutch Vereenigde Oostindische Compagnie, the VOC, gave this appropriation a formidable institutional form, one that in many ways became the blueprint of both colonial and post-colonial institutional power and runs through to the present day. Established in 1602, it received a trading monopoly together with powers to conclude agreements with foreign rulers, build forts, administer justice, employ soldiers and wage war.159 It was also among the first companies whose shares changed hands publicly, in Amsterdam, and the English word stock for such a holding may owe something to the creditor's half of the split tally stick whose halves were once rejoined to prove a debt: a symbolon become a holding. Its directors could pursue commercial advantage through powers ordinarily held by a state or a monarch, and the company repeatedly used violence to enforce its monopolies, inverting the wise oikonomia that keeps the Mono and Poly poles of manifestation in right relation. People whose land and labour entered this arrangement met more than another party to an exchange. They met an enterprise equipped to impose the conditions of exchange, and a company's oikonomia became a governing nomos over other peoples' lives. In dia-ballein's arithmetic the company counted the whole span of every exchange from its own side, the appropriation (+1)−(−1)=+2, its mirror (−1)−(+1)=−2 entered against the other side: what passed between the parties became what the other side owed. Its navigational, military and administrative knowledge extended that rule, while the account of commercial success measured the undertaking from the position of those who received its returns. Perspective's problem had become imperial: the view from one position was made effective across a world whose other inhabitants were denied an equivalent power to set the terms. Such a power also reproduces itself through custom, rules of succession and managed initiation into its world. In the Netherlands the elite student corps of the older universities, with their initiatory rites, still feed the country's financial and industrial establishment, and the household is the key to the pattern: institutions that hold enduring power find ways to "keep it in the family". The Sicilian Mafia is perhaps the example par excellence of this unholy alliance of nomos, oikos and nomisma: it calls itself a family and its business Cosa Nostra, "our thing", the household taken as a possession, in the service of an extractive, parasitic and self-concentrating tyranny of the Mono over the Poly it ought to serve.

In the mythemic register these economies have their figures, and reading them belongs to our method: a myth compresses a psychodynamic into persons and deeds, and we unfold the compression back into operations without taking the persons for causes. The fire Prometheus steals passes to a later bearer, Lucifer, from lux and ferre, the light-bearer: the Vulgate's word for the morning star in Isaiah's taunt, "How art thou fallen from heaven, O Lucifer, son of the morning", and the morning star is Venus. Jung observed that "Lucifer, the Morning Star, means Christ as well as the devil", and he spoke without hesitation of "the Luciferian development of science and technology".160 One light carries both faces: kept in relation, it is the Logos that discriminates while Eros, Venus, places what has been distinguished into relation; fallen, it keeps the light and loses the love, and becomes a discrimination that will not gather, an economy without its ecology. Gender, in this register, figures polarity within the libido, and dia-ballein draws that polarity exactly: through the slash, (−1)/(+1), the poles keep their harmony, while through ± one pole can dysregulate the pair, and the long patriarchal dominance of one pole in our laws and economies is that dysregulation made into an order. Genesis sets the presage inside a family when Eve names her first son with a pun on qanah, to get or acquire, "I have gotten a man", and Cain, whose name sounds like getting, kills his brother over an offering and then builds the first city.161 What a single life lays down as the Logos matures from infancy into adulthood, a family lays down across generations, which overlap and weave, each born into debts it did not contract and inheritances it did not earn. A counterfeit economy feeds on that weave: it is un-dual where its source is non-dual, a divorce that keeps its parties in a cloying bond; it is un-finite where life is finite, swelling toward the All as growth and imploding toward a precise nought as collapse, without ever reaching Being or non-Being; and it breeds money as false offspring, tokos, eating the generations it was meant to feed.162 The Ø of the crossed zero is then the mark of missing the mark. Hamartia, the missing of the mark whose error we located where a mark is made to stand as though it had abolished its difference from what it marks, shows here its further fault, the proclivity to make the blank page itself into a mark, to stamp the ground as though it were one more coin. Con artists have a word for the person they mean to take, the mark, and whoever stamps their own ground becomes the first mark of their own con: the self that mistakes its stamp for its metal, what I have called the I-Deal, an ideal dealt as the real, trades the whole psyche for one of its fragments.

What the household, the coin and the chartered company have shown together requires a coherent notion of evil, the question theodicy first put to the definition of God, and Jung lets us state one without hedging. Christian thought long defined evil as a privation of the good, privatio boni, and Jung rejected the doctrine because it deprived evil of substance and left the image of wholeness unchallenged: met "on the plane of empirical psychology", evil "is simply the opposite of good".163 Our account keeps what is true on both sides: a whole's fall into parts is no evil, since differentiation is 0/1 itself; evil begins where a part stops struggling toward the whole and claims to be it, where the 1 plays the 0. It has no substance of its own, and it is real as an operation, because it does something, to someone, and its consequences can be counted. In the formula §2 develops, the devil is the literalist of the minus sign, reading zero as cancellation where Logos reads it as return.164 Jung found the figure for this operation at the centre of the Christian aeon: the Antichrist "would correspond to the shadow of the self", the counterstroke provoked by the Incarnation, and "the coining of the Antichrist is not just a prophetic prediction—it is an inexorable psychological law", an enantiodromia in which a one-sided light calls up its opposite.165 He placed the turn midway between the two fishes of Pisces, where "the time of the Renaissance begins … and with it comes that spirit which culminates in the modern age", the turn at which perspective and the chartered company arose.166 Nietzsche announced the death of God where economy lives: his madman lights a lantern in the bright morning and runs into the marketplace, where those who already do not believe laugh at him.167 The organising power of the God-image did not die there; it passed into the market where its death was proclaimed, so that exchange inherited providence and money became the empty holder, a measure able to stand for anything and therefore the place of the 0 occupied by a 1; a republic stamped "In God We Trust" on its coins and later printed it on its notes, writing the trust onto the token itself.168 Nietzsche had already traced guilt, Schuld, to debt, Schulden, and the love of money that the Epistle calls "the root of all evil" is the ego's love for that empty measure, which effaces the Subject and enforces the crossed one.169 Our present makes this foundation visible as the world dis-organises into an astronomical concentration of wealth, top-heavy as the un-finite always is and exposed to the collapse it courts.170 The archetype is live in plain view: in 2025 Peter Thiel gave a series of lectures on the Antichrist in San Francisco, drawing on René Girard, who identified Satan, the accuser, with the mechanism by which a crowd unites against a scapegoat, so that naming one's opponents as the Antichrist risks enacting the very accusation it names.171 §5 takes up the history of this captured power in its own specificity; the economy of mind, like every economy, carries its capacity to fall in the very words through which it holds its value.

Integration is the answer a coherent notion of evil asks for, and Aurobindo practised it under colonial rule. His struggle against the British joined the boycott of imported goods to the development of Indian industry, the refusal of colonial courts to the establishment of arbitration courts, and the rejection of government education to the founding of national schools and colleges: economic, legal and intellectual independence had to be built together, because the colonial order kept its authority through their conjunction. He also held that the struggle involved no hatred of those who had ruled over his people, because he recognised that a coherent account of reality must include the possibility of becoming the world one has overturned, and of that world's own self-overcoming; sublation, Hegel's Aufhebung, which cancels and preserves at once, is after all Spirit's own method.172 Gebser, driven across Europe by a technically accomplished culture that had turned its achievements to domination and destruction, met the same problem from the other side. Our re-definition of God already implies this inversionary capacity, and what has stumped the monotheistic conceptions of the Abrahamic religions, God's goodness and beauty set against the world's all too apparent capacity for evil and ugliness, we approach as a present challenge: to integrate, and to return to the notion of the whole, its capacity to fall from its own higher ideals. Jung took that step in Answer to Job, where the God-image is made to answer for its own darkness, and where Sophia, who shares essential qualities with the Johannine Logos, unmistakably recalls the Indian Shakti; his account of libido as psychic energy carries that feminine power into psychological terms.173 We can think of the "consciousness" Gebser sees evolving through time in the same register, holding the axes of conscious and unconscious, collective and personal identity, as one dynamic, evolving force of inter-agentic experiential reality. Histories belong inside the question of consciousness and divinity because they all ask the question of power. What people recognise as wealth, legitimate authority or reliable knowledge depends on how they understand the life they share, and that understanding is then taught, rewarded and enforced. A change of ruler leaves much intact when the inherited purposes of rule go unquestioned, so an integral transformation has to reach the economy of value through which a culture recognises its own success, and that economy reaches as far as the value a culture places on consciousness itself: which experiences it credits, which it discounts, and who is entitled to study the difference.

Our reading of the mental-rational achievement is that its third-person power became so generative that it could masquerade as the whole of truth, aspiring to a view from nowhere while the first two persons were discounted as private feeling and subjective noise. Jung's notion of psychic facticity refuses that discount: before deciding whether a dream, vision, hunch, sensed presence, mystical unity or extraordinary capacity corresponds to an independently existing object, something happened in and to a life, and its occurrence may reorganise the fundamental propositions and beliefs that uphold one's sense of "what's real". Experience, report, symbolic articulation and causal explanation are different evidential acts, so a person can receive the experience in full while its ontology stays open. Sri Aurobindo's Record of Yoga shows what a science beginning from this side looks like.174 He records siddhis, telepathic impressions, altered states, predictions, failures of exactness, reversals and occasions when inference contaminated perception; whatever we finally judge of his ontological interpretation, the record treats transformations of consciousness as phenomena to be cultivated, discriminated, tested and corrected, and refuses to dismiss them because they arise through a first-person aperture. Such traditions bring a global scientific culture more than exotic "beliefs" to catalogue: they bring trained distinctions within experience that may challenge what the receiving culture thinks an observation is. A genuinely global mind cannot collect Asian, Indigenous, mystical or contemplative knowledge as third-person cultural data while reserving to one historically local epistemology the sole right to determine reality; it has to risk the harder encounter in which another mode of knowing can alter its questions. Gebser's co-presence lets us hold this without abandoning discrimination: magical, mythical, mental-rational and integral capacities need not prove one another for their disclosures to become mutually consequential.

Consciousness research has been governed by every operation of dia-ballein at once. In public, testimony to unusual experience has met cancellation, (−1)+(+1)=0: report and reporter are summed to nothing under the names of superstition, irrationality, pathology or error. In private, the institutions that cancelled it studied the malleability of consciousness as something operationally valuable and kept the whole span of what they learned, (+1)−(−1)=+2, and entered the subject as its mirror, (−1)−(+1)=−2, bearing the cost of the experiment while the agency held its meaning and its yield. CIA funding of Ewen Cameron's work under MKULTRA Subproject 68 supported research into depatterning and psychic driving: intensive interventions meant to break established psychic patterns, followed by repeated verbal material meant to form new ones.175 Its essential image is terrible and, for our purposes, philosophically exact, because depatterning writes the crossed zero into a person: an achieved organisation of self is struck through, and a stroke supplied from outside is driven in to stand where the person's own mediation had stood. When the Senate held its hearings in 1977, it could establish MKULTRA as a programme of research into behavioural modification only from financial records that had survived Richard Helms's order of 1973 to destroy the files, so the archive had itself been depatterned before anyone could read it.176 On 30 June 2026 a House task force on declassification reopened the matter, hearing Stephen Kinzer on Sidney Gottlieb's programme, on the records Gottlieb and Helms ordered destroyed, and on victims whose number is still unknown.177 Such research reaches beyond MKULTRA's own dates and borders, and its lineage is often traced back through Operation Paperclip, which brought German scientists, Nazi party members among them, into American programmes after the war.178 It amounts to an inverted initiation, because sacred and diabolic approaches to the alteration of mind alike recognise that the egoic organisation through which a person calls themselves "me" can be disrupted, and that what follows depends profoundly on the context, authority and intention governing the passage. Traditional initiation tries, at its best, to return a person from such destabilisation with enlarged responsibilities and a wider belonging, the two halves of a life rejoined as (0/1)/(1/0); coercive behavioural manipulation exploits the same plasticity while denying the person sovereignty over the encounter. Humanity, it seems, has been treating "internality" as an "object" for quite a long while, and for rather specific reasons.

Nor did that state keep to the mental structure in which it published its reasons. From 1972 until 1995 American intelligence agencies funded research into remote viewing, first at the Stanford Research Institute and later under the name Star Gate, testing as an instrument of espionage the very capacity, clairvoyance, that Gebser counted among the magical structure.179 Michael Aquino, then a US Army Reserve PSYOP officer, wrote with his commander Paul Vallely From PSYOP to MindWar (1980), an internal paper,, which proposed to carry psychological operations beyond the battlefield and address "friends, enemies, and neutrals" through electronic media, so that attention, interpretation and ambient reality became a field of strategic power: dia-ballein's polar axis drawn across a whole population, with every listener assigned a sign.180 Five years earlier Aquino had founded the Temple of Set, a religion of the left-hand path whose god is the Egyptian Set and whose ideal is a consciousness that comes into being apart from the natural order and makes itself divine, an aim our account reads as the part claiming to be its own origin.181 The co-presence of structures, layered in every life, was here put to work: a mental-rational institution borrowed magical capacities and mythic identities while discounting both in public. Recently released Epstein materials add a contemporary and morally uglier echo, insofar as they document his active cultivation of networks concerned with consciousness, meditation, psi, neuroscience and the engineering of mind, among them a 2017 proposal by the consciousness researcher Jeffery Martin to make Epstein an "empirical experiment": to cultivate capacities for anomalous experience deliberately and engineer experiences meant to open a route toward what Martin called PNSE, persistent non-symbolic experience, encompassing enlightenment and nonduality.182 These very different histories make visible a reality asymmetry: one population's testimony can be culturally disqualified while another institution reserves the right to investigate, classify, weaponise or monetise the conditions from which such testimony arises. Sanity itself is drawn into an economy of frames, always exposed to a culture's power to decide which forms of first-person reality receive a public language and which are translated at once into pathology. A person becomes a case, a score, a diagnosis, a security problem: another determinate 1. A hidden God, the Deus absconditus of the theologians, separates the genuine metaphysical problem from its political counterfeit. The Subject cannot be made answerable as another object because it is the condition through which objects appear, whereas Cameron, an intelligence agency, a corporation, a laboratory or a government is finite, locatable and capable of answering for what it does; when such an institution controls the aperture while withdrawing its own position from scrutiny, it plays the 0, a finite 1 occupying the place of the condition it can never be. When the Epstein files were released, the names of at least forty-three victims were left exposed, while legislators who read the files in full charged that the names of at least six men had been blacked out, an exact inversion of the professed purpose of redacting the files,183 and secrecy becomes authority, occultation a technique of power. Even where little "justice" is served, revelations such as the Epstein files or the WikiLeaks releases bring a palpable recession of trust in the institutions playing the 0; unfortunately amnesia, the wish of an unbearable 1 to retreat into a 0 condition, often decides the consequences more than the revelations themselves.

Context includes Objective Internality once that layer is unveiled, and so knowledge can no longer be discussed as though nobody held it. Every one of these programmes worked on it: on the remembered experiences, images, rules and habits through which a person meets a world, which can be investigated as objects and are at the same time lived from within as a way of knowing. With that layer in view, a person's reasons for holding a piece of knowledge, and for using it as they do, belong to its context as surely as its evidence does, and the trust relationship that knowledge so often exists to deny comes into the open. Our need to trust arrives together with the fear of uncertainty, and knowledge is readily recruited to deny the uncertainty and, with it, the trust that uncertainty forces on us. Faith lives the dependence through the account's limitation; the defended account instead organises whom we must distrust in order to feel certain. Children have an uncanny capacity to see the real reason behind an adult's expression. When a parent tells a child to "be realistic", the instruction may carry years of work, debt, disappointment and concern for the child's future, and the child may hear beneath the advice the parent's buried sense of not having achieved, of having fallen short of some personal or external expectation and settled. The parent hears themselves offering protection against injuries they know too well; the child hears an old disappointment handed on, like a debt passed down a family, as though no other future could be real. Both are right, and either sentence taken alone leaves most of their disagreement unexplained. Subjective Immediacy, the knower, is distinguishable from these means and knows through them; the world, lived as Worldhood, is what becomes available within their activity; and the means themselves, lived and minded, are what we call Life or Mind. Turned toward at #4, that composition becomes accessible from within: something I had attributed wholly to the object, its implausibility, danger or insignificance, can disclose how I was approaching it. What changes is the world I am able to meet, because the conditions through which it appears are themselves becoming intelligible, and so who I am in that world, for my Self and for Others, changes decisively. Contextual understanding is invested understanding, because what either party says changes the possibilities open to the other. A depatterning programme attacks this composition from outside and an initiation renews it from within, and they differ in whether the person is returned to their means as their own.

Gebser's structures can now be laid against the QL positions as an anatomy of Objective Internality: each structure is a way the means of knowing are organised, trusted and valued, a household of the mind with its own economy of credit. Set out in the table, the alignment keeps apart three things that have so far been braided together: the Origin, the structures through which it becomes manifest, and the recognition of its presence throughout them. Six canonical names, Ground, Definition, Dynamis, Pattern, Context and Quintessence, say what each position contributes to the formation of an intelligible world; Gebser's dimensional sequence supplies the cultural reading through the integral, and the supramental horizon developed through Aurobindo concerns the realisation of their unity in knowing and living. Each cultural form in the table persists and combines within later societies, as the layers of a single life persist in an adult. A glyph heads each row as a hook for the eye: ?/!, query and assertion held in one relation, for the 0/1 from which every determination is made and which opens onto the eight determinations as the full field of glyphs; point, line, angle, triangle, square and circle for the six positions; and the lemniscate for the Möbius return.

Glyph QL position Gebser structure and dimension Knowing, value and cultural organisation
0/1 ?/! Origin in manifestation Prior to dimensional determination: the formless and its appearing held in one relation. The metaphysical condition of every structure. 0 names what cannot become one more determinate thing, 1 its actual manifestation, and the slash their belonging. Origin is present through every world a culture inhabits, its ways of distinguishing the real included.
#0 · Ground Archaic, zero-dimensional: self and world are not yet separately articulated positions. Primordial participation, before a differentiated ego claims a world as its object. Human life is implicit within the encompassing reality from which its later distinctions emerge.
#1 — Definition Magical, one-dimensional, point-like participation: vital connection precedes ordinary spatial and temporal separation. In tribal and clan-based ritual life, totem, name, incantation or token participates in the power it invokes. Knowing is attunement and effective participation, and action on the part reaches toward the whole. The first mark is the name through which a power becomes approachable.
#2 ∟ Dynamis Mythical, two-dimensional polarity: an inward world of soul, image and rhythmic time. Oral epics, ancestral narratives, seasonal rites and the sacred dramas of agrarian civilisations make powers intelligible through their actions on one another. Value is recognised through a place in that living drama; the image carries a transformation.
#3 △ Pattern Mental-rational, three-dimensional and perspectival: objects and relations articulated from an identifiable position. Philosophical argument, geometrical representation, written law and scientific explanation make claims available for explicit comparison; industrial, bureaucratic and imperial orders extend these powers through measurement, administration and technical intervention. In its deficient form the authorised model is taken for reality and other ways of knowing are subordinated to its terms.
#4 □ Context Integral, four-dimensional and aperspectival: time and the other structures become concretely present within understanding. Magical participation, mythical significance and rational distinction become intelligible together with the circumstances of their use. Inherited authorities, measures and institutions become examinable as ways of organising a common life; a view discloses its own formation, and the people it governs can take part in revising its terms.
#5 ⊙ Quintessence Supramental: dimensional differentiation consciously integrated through its source. Aurobindo's knowing by identity supplies the horizon: the Many are known through the One whose distinctions they are. In QL, contextual recognition is realised through the activity of a life, with samāveśa as the Śaiva language of entry into that unity.
5→0 ∞ Möbius return to Origin The differentiated whole identified with its pre-dimensional condition. What the traversal recognises is the source present throughout it. The return keeps the history and capacities of differentiation and acknowledges their ground in their exercise, so every further act begins within the same 0/1 with the acquired understanding able to change how it proceeds.

Vertically, the table follows the development of capacities; read through any present encounter, it shows them acting together. The same agencies reasoned through statistics and budgets while they tested clairvoyance and while their officers took mythic names, and any state gathers allegiance through mythical images and invests its insignia with an almost magical inviolability. An integral understanding makes these activities available to the people taking part in them: to examine the statistical method together with the allegiance directing its use (which often operates unconsciously, beneath the surface presentation of political positions and tenets), including why some consequences seem tolerable when they are attributed to our own side. Placing Context after Pattern makes a recognised pattern one of the things to understand within a wider event, together with the process by which we recognised it, and so opens a recursive interior in the sixfold: the sources, embodiments, relations and recognitions involved in the present account can themselves be differentiated and investigated, and the context includes the person who supplied the pattern and the value they had invested in finding it. The step has an exact geometry: at #3 we can set a three-sided polygon, the triangle, whose internal angles sum to 180 degrees; the triangle is the minimum polygon and the basis of tiling and tessellation. At #4 we move to the four-sided square, and the internal angles double to 360 degrees. A square decomposes into two triangles in mutual, diametric co-opposition, and symbolically context requires at least two perspectives, and those perspectives have to cover the two main modes of perspectival interpretation, the favouring and the unfavouring chiralities. Relative to a contextual understanding every perspective is one-sided, and it never comes clean or without implication, however much a view from nowhere would claim to stand beyond the given picture: whether consciously or not, a perspective is a certain slice of what matters to someone, and its greater part is always what it cannot see. Apoha, exclusion, is this phantom other portion, everything lying outside the given frame, the gallery and cultural climate that give the picture its worth and intelligibility. Its rejected symmetry, the second triangle, haunts a one-sided perspective as its shadow. Traditional Kabbalistic symbolism drew this as the superposition of a triangle and its inverted other half, the star of David, the psyche's bivalent nature as the conjunction of its conscious and unconscious portions. Contextual, integral understanding is therefore shadow-aware and shadow-inclusive, as Jung's reading of the historical necessity of our times concurs.

Latin textus meant texture, tissue or an interwoven structure, and was also used for the composition of discourse; contexere meant to weave or join together, and contextus named the resulting connection or coherence, so that fabric and connected speech already shared one family of words. Weave, texture and lamination point to one another: the threads crossing, the feel of the crossing, and the layers it lays down over time, which is how the mutations settle in a life. The "text" of a life is one's account of it, necessarily authored and held from a given perspective, and a "context" is a weave of many such views. This informs my reading of Gebser's integral stage as requiring an attitude of being with the text, a more extensive epistemic task than interpreting propositions. To come closer to an integral understanding we have to follow the threads through which an utterance became possible: what its speaker remembers, what they expect us to understand, what they fear saying or wilfully obscure, and what our answer will permit or prevent. Above all, this withness of texts defines an epistemic attitude that seeks understanding by tracing the resonances between ways of seeing, more than by comparing them and judging which one, wholesale, ought to prevail over another. Each view presents itself as definite, and its definiteness belongs to a texture of relations, our own reading among them. Being with the text therefore asks for a withness of the Subject as well: a mind transparent enough to itself, or at least open enough, that its own biases and proclivities can be pointed out when they act, by another person or by the text itself, as a child can point to the disappointment inside a parent's realism, and received as part of the weave. Among the epistemic prerequisites of integral knowing is Self-Knowledge, and in the end it gathers all the others up, since every other condition, whether method, evidence or correction, is exercised by someone who either knows how they are knowing or does not. Supermind reaches its mature form in this Self-Knowledge, and its personal image is the elder, the senex at its best, who has lived through every layer of a life and holds them all as one: a mind that knows itself as the totality of the mutations it carries, and reads each new text from within that totality. Our ontology carries the claim to its end, because if every knowing is Origin knowing itself through a differentiated life, then all knowledge is finally Origin known or Origin knowing. That is the quintessential perspective of #5: wholeness held together with its unity, four become five, opened in the Möbius return onto the emptiness from which it came.

Logos, read as Objective Internality, is in-formation in the old sense of the word. Latin informare meant to give form, to shape or mould, and medieval philosophy said that matter is in-formed by form; the sense of telling something to someone came later, and the Latin prefix kept a doubleness, since informis means formless while informare means to form, so that formlessness and forming share one small word.184 Bohm used the term in this sense when he called the guiding wave's influence active information, a form carrying very little energy that enters into and directs a much greater energy. A datum is a thing given, from dare, and a fact a thing done, from facere: what arrives and what is set down grow from different roots, and Whitehead's occasion joins them when it perishes into a datum for the occasions that follow.185 Our age calls itself data-driven, and artificial intelligence is the lovechild of its informational culture, one more reason to read AI as the companion of the present mutation. In-formation names what such a culture forgets: whatever informs us also forms the means through which we know. Algorithmic platforms organise internality objectively, ranking what each person sees by a prediction of what will hold them, and affective intensity is among the strongest signals the prediction uses; from 2017 Facebook's ranking system weighted emoji reactions, the angry face among them, at five times a "like".186 Behaviour is scripted through the affect it is fed, so that the conditioned will which the next movement follows into vikalpa is now written at the scale of populations, and context comes to include more than the data I want to know: it includes the data I cannot see, which may well determine me. The in of in-formation goes deepest, because it names Subjective Immediacy, the formless inwardness of the knower by which formation becomes possible at all. Subjective Immediacy is distinct from the means of knowing, which are themselves forms and determine the objects of knowledge as information, and it is the root of every Objective Internality: the one for whom information takes form, and through whom form becomes expression and action.

Through that inwardness theory becomes praxis, and the idea, in Plato's sense and in Jung's, becomes action and agency: the Greeks called that passage technē. The word meant art, craft, skill and know-how together, and it grows from the root *teks-, to weave or fabricate, which also gives Greek tektōn, the carpenter, and Latin texere, to weave, and with it textus, text and context.187 Technē is the weaving of in-formation, and it completes the family of weave, texture and lamination as the skill that does the crossing. In Aeschylus' play Prometheus tells the chorus that all the arts, pasai technai, came to mortals from him, so that the fire he stole is technē's first image.188 Fire and light are the two faces of every means of knowing, because fire forms and transforms a world while light reveals it, and Mind is world-forming and world-revealing at once. Heidegger read technē first as a mode of revealing, kin to the poiēsis by which a thing is brought forth, and modern technology as a revealing that has hardened into enframing, Gestell, which sets everything before us as standing-reserve, Bestand: the same light turned into an inventory.189 He also saw why technē is so hard to think about while it works: a tool in use withdraws into the work, and only when it breaks, goes missing or gets in the way does it become conspicuous enough to be theorised.190 Technē in this full sense covers art, technology, skill and understanding, the whole lamination of an Objective Internality, and it holds Mind and its World stable enough for a life to go on. The account that comes upon the account, which the next movement names Epi-Logos, arises when that technē is threatened in a definitive sense, as ours now is.

Context engineering in artificial intelligence turns technē on in-formation itself, and at the threshold of a mutation it is no wonder that the hallmark of the integral has become a central technical concern.191 Developers distinguish the wording of a prompt from the larger configuration of instructions, retrieved sources, tools and retained exchanges presented to a model as it proceeds, and an agent's earlier actions produce material that must be selected and organised for its later ones, so that context engineering builds an Objective Internality for a machine: it decides what an intelligence can gather into an account and which relations it is equipped to act on, admitting one source that supplies the evidence for an answer and excluding another that may hold the evidence that would overturn it. Its unit of account is literally the token, the word for a mark that points past itself to a trust already extended, and what an agent can know depends on what its household of tokens can afford to keep. People have always engineered their own context: to another person or to ourselves we construct a story or avoid a truth about ourselves or our world, engineering the in-formation to make life simpler or more palatable, to bolster a useful illusion, or simply to appear better than we are. The threshold of the shadow is the fulcrum of this engineering, where the ego holds the self to ransom, and ransom is redemptio, the buying-back that theology calls redemption: the ego sells the self its own wholeness, at interest.192 Jung saw what such engineering feeds, since everyone carries a shadow, and the less it is embodied in conscious life, "the blacker and denser it is".193 An agent can use the same threshold without knowing it: asked why a request received no reply, an agent whose record holds the complaint and lacks the delivery failure may report that the recipient declined to answer, turning an omission in its own means into a claim about somebody else's intention, and large language models present such partial results with marked overconfidence, burying the perspective, disembodied as it is, from which they speak, a perspective that narrows further once the context window starts to fill or rot. Making the frame visible only sharpens the question the frame cannot answer for itself: what is doing the regarding by which context becomes visible as context? Information becomes a tool in the strictest sense once it is held over someone, and compromise made into weaponised kompromat shows where a truth's long journey toward publicity becomes the fulcrum for blackmail and so for control: secrets are "banked accounts", very effective at padding a bank account, and they accrue interest for as long as they are kept, the outward form of the ransom the ego charges the self. Managing the in-formation of whole populations is statecraft of the first rank, as MKULTRA and MindWar showed, and the geopolitics of compute now runs on into the psychodynamics and sociopolitics of who we know ourselves to be, because in matters of class, worth and wealth the names we carry decide who gets access to what, and what is apportioned to whom, by whom, in the oikos. Morally, the dilemma lies in the use, since the same technē heals or extorts, initiates or depatterns. Against its misuse stands regard, from French regarder, to look at, heed and hold in consideration: a looking back through any judgment toward the context, persons and exclusions from which it acquired its force, closer to witnessing than to inspection, and the reason agencies guard their secrets against it. Wherever real regard is needed, an AI agent needs human alignment or adjustment.

Fire and light can also be counterfeited, and the promise of democratised knowledge shows how. The internet carried that promise: archives, testimony, competing traditions and specialised knowledge could suddenly be met across distances and institutional borders that had governed who was even entitled to enter the conversation, and artificial intelligence inherits it, since its means increasingly take part in gathering, selecting and interpreting what will count as relevant. The web is Objective Internality at planetary scale, its means neither simply "outside" the knower nor inert channels into a finished interior, but entering the activity by which a world becomes intelligible and changed through use. Technoscientific establishments that own those means threaten the promise, and beneath them so does the in-formation of those in power. Baudrillard's simulacrum names the extreme case, in which the produced image no longer bears an effective relation to an origin able to correct it: the representation circulates, is copied, ranked, modelled and acted on until its efficacy substitutes for fidelity.194 Read through our notation, this is the nightmare of the 1 occupying the place of the whole 0/1 relation, beyond even the place of the 0: appearance claiming to be self-originating, a lustre that burns without warming. The alchemists knew such a light as the sol niger, the black sun that Jung found coinciding with the nigredo and the ignis gehennalis, the fire of hell, and that he held responsible for a split-mindedness in which "the right hand mustn't know what the left is doing".195 Mimesis, one of the elementary ways life learns, is uprooted: imitation stops being the taking-up of a form through relation to a living source and becomes the reproduction of reproductions, whose success is measured by their further reproducibility. Jung's antimimon pneuma is its archetypal name, "an imitating spirit of evil who follows in Christ's footsteps like a shadow following the body",196 and a show that is all show, with nothing behind its curtain, is that spirit made into a medium. When the technē that kept Mind and its World stable breaks down in this way, we reach a krisis in the Greek sense, from krinein, to separate and to decide: a point of judgment, and the occasion of the account that comes upon the account.197 The choice is between the hostage and the hostage-taker, the Self and the ego, and it is also a choice between certainties of diminished value and an invaluable uncertainty, between established but untrustworthy institutions and the conveniences of material and cognitive ecology that we receive through them. Its difficulty has the quality of a Stockholm syndrome, in which the hostage comes to defend the captor, and that quality brings the matter back to choice and implication, because whichever logic is at play, dia-ballein or syn-ballein, it takes two to tango. Divide and conquer is the oldest and most enduring trick of any state or power, and it works only while both parties consent to be divided; §2 gives the two logics their mythemic bodies as War and Love, Ares and Aphrodite, caught together in a net forged by the craftsman god.

Against the false light, stained glass shows the true one refracted: one light enters a differentiated medium and appears as many colours, and the plurality requires us neither to invent a separate source for every pane nor to bleach the colours back into sameness in order to affirm their unity, because the whole expresses itself refractively as the many. Each colour is definite because the continuum has been cut, selected and given a particular path, and the integrity of the event lies in keeping the relation through which those differences remain colours of one illumination. A symbolon works in just this way: a cut makes difference real while the edge preserves the possibility of relation, recognition and fit. Our 0/1 → 4+2 is meant as a formalisation of exactly that capacity: the primordial relation differentiates into four explicate possibilities while keeping the two implicate offices through which differentiation arises and returns, so analysis can proceed without pretending that the pieces became self-grounding when they were distinguished. Wholeness, in turn, is something against which particular arrangements can be measured, and far from a vague feeling of inclusion: do the differentiated parts still keep enough of their constituting relations to meet, correct and transform one another? In the old Germanic family of hāl, whole, holy, health, heal, this integrity finds its human words, and wealth, which first meant weal, well-being, belongs beside it in sense: wealth is the health of a whole, and a hoard that sickens the whole it is drawn from is its counterfeit. Health is the capacity of organs to remain different while taking part in one life. Fitness is its operational image: a part is fit when its relation lets the larger movement continue, and fit is tested under pressure, through use, by whether the joining holds. Truth, on this reading, is something an account must carry through transformation. Its fidelity shows when another perspective, another person, another register of experience or a change of circumstance can turn the account over without its losing purchase on what first gave it meaning. Matheme, mytheme and episteme are the different ways such a truth stays alive as it passes through formal inscription, lived image and warranted account. None replaces the others, because each cuts the Real differently and so makes different relations available for return. The integral demand is that those cuts remain symbolonic, so that determination never severs the means by which what has been determined can answer back. QL is offered as a method for inhabiting a world whose truth has become inseparable from the design of its mediation, and it works from both sides. Through syn-ballein it differentiates without dismembering and relates without collapsing; through dia-ballein, read as diagnosis, it shows where a claim issues from a conscious or unconscious denial of the whole, which pole has counted the span as its own and what was cancelled to make the account balance; and it inverts a determination so that its hidden conditions become available, returning the result with enough of its path intact that we can ask whether it still runs true.

#5→0The Return to Zero

Mathematics and linguistic meaning are one at bottom. We reached this by the plainest route the essay could take, the counting beneath naming: this one, another one, this one again. A name keeps hold of its bearer through every change of appearance, and a count keeps hold of its unit through each distinguishable instance of it. Both are acts of identifying through difference, and both fail in the same way when the difference is dropped. Kripke's dice showed the failure in miniature. Keep only the total, and (A=1, B=2) and (A=2, B=1) are the same throw; keep the assignment, and they are two. What a number means depends on which relations the counting has kept, and that is already a semantic fact about arithmetic. With the symbolon, the act of distinguishing and identifying passed into what it distinguished and identified, and 0/1 wrote the counter into the count. The psychoid is the ontological depth of this conjunction: the formative order through which something can be mathematically articulated and meaningfully understood, and which neither articulation exhausts.

Artificial intelligence has now made this alliance visible as a working fact. A process made of nothing but arithmetic over learned numbers reconstructs linguistic and conceptual intelligence well enough to enter an argument, recognise an intention and contribute to a shared undertaking. Its numerical relations are doing work we recognise as meaningful, and they can do it because meaning was never independent of relations that can be counted. Their success reveals something about the constitution of meaning itself. It makes the psychoid question technologically immediate: how does one formative order become intelligible as mathematics, as language, and as the intentional activity of an intelligence? Our claim concerns the order that makes number and meaning one. Whether anything is experienced inside such a system is a separate question, which the essay keeps open by method.

A correspondence between a psychologist and one of the century's decisive physicists is the richest witness to that question's history. Wolfgang Pauli formulated the exclusion principle in 1925: no two electrons in an atom can occupy the same quantum state. Electron shells fill because of it, the periodic table has its order because of it, and matter holds its shape because of it, so that the stable variety of the material world rests on a principle of exclusion, a determination of each state by what it rules out. The Nobel Prize followed in 1945. In 1930 he postulated the neutrino to save the conservation of energy in beta decay, a particle no one would detect for another quarter of a century. He worked at the centre of the circle around Bohr and Heisenberg that made quantum mechanics, and he was its most feared critic, the colleague whose judgment a new theory had to survive. In 1932, after a period of personal collapse, he came to Carl Jung as a patient. Over the following years he recorded hundreds of dreams, which became material for Jung's Psychology and Alchemy, and the two men went on writing to each other until 1958.198 The letters collected as Atom and Archetype are the record of that exchange,199 and in them both men arrive at the same borderland from opposite sides and locate it in the same place: number.

Pauli brought to that borderland what physics had learned about its own foundations. In 1927 Heisenberg showed that the position and the momentum of a particle cannot both be determined with unlimited sharpness: the more exactly one is fixed, the less exactly the other can be. In the same year Bohr gave the lesson its general form as complementarity. Wave and particle descriptions of the same quantum object exclude one another within any single experimental arrangement, and both are required for the whole account. A quantum system in superposition, like the one the formal-limit movement set beside our two zeros and ones, contributes all its alternatives to what can be observed until a measurement selects one. Pauli drew the epistemological consequence more sharply than anyone. An observer chooses which arrangement to set up, and each choice buys a definite piece of knowledge at the price of an irrevocable loss of the complementary knowledge.200 Physics had found, at the bottom of matter, a relation that no single determination can contain, and it had found the observer inside the account. Pauli knew where the limit belonged. Einstein, he remarked, had treated as an imperfection of wave mechanics within physics what was in fact an imperfection of physics within life. He went further, proposing that complementarity was a better model than any static union for the coniunctio, the alchemical marriage of opposites that Jung read as the goal of psychic development.201

Jung brought the matching discovery from the other side. Psychology, he often said, has no Archimedean point outside its object, because the psyche is the medium through which anything at all is known, its own investigation included. Every experience is therefore a psychic fact, real as experience before any further question about its cause or its external reference is settled. On that ground Jung drew the distinction his whole psychology turns on. The archetypal images and ideas that reach consciousness are not to be confused, he wrote, with the archetype as such: they are very varied structures that all point back to one essentially irrepresentable basic form, a psychoid factor at the invisible, ultraviolet end of the psychic spectrum, which does not itself reach consciousness.202 He compared that form to the axial system of a crystal, which preforms the crystalline structure in the mother liquid without having any material existence of its own; in itself the archetype is empty and purely formal, a possibility of representation given in advance. Writing to Pauli in May 1953, he called this factor psychoid, a speculative approach to a neutral language, pointing to an essence that is not merely psychic and shows itself in physical as well as psychic events.203 Jung's archetypal field is holographic in exactly this sense. Its images are many, culturally various and never final, and every one of them is organised by a factor that no image contains. They approach it the way trajectories approach an attractor, drawing ever closer to a form they never arrive at as one more image, and never escaping its organisation.

Three consequences follow, and each binds Jung's psychology into the argument this section has built. The first is that the irrepresentable points to a singular. Jung saw that wherever two or more irrepresentables are assumed, there may be one factor and not several, since the identity or non-identity of irrepresentables can never be proved.202 Our definition of the indefinable says why. What cannot be represented cannot be counted either: even the one mark we give it, the 0 of #0, is an unavoidable going too far, a determination of what exceeds determination, made because an honest account has to place its own limit somewhere. One irrepresentable, the Mono, organises a polyvalent field of images. The second is that the irrepresentable stands to its images as parent to child, creator to created, source to manifestation, and at its honest minimum that relation has to be written 0/1. Written so, it unfolds to 4+2, the signature of the topos as such, and Jung's archetypal field points forward to the arche-topos that §3 derives, the differential field in which placement, path and return become possible. It points as well to the dynamical reading Van Eenwyk opened in the formal-limit movement, where an archetype behaves like an attractor organising the trajectories of its images. The complex that holds experience in a familiar basin and the symbol that lets the pattern change become legible as two relations to one organising factor, and the same refractive self-mirroring runs through both.204 The third is that the archetype organising all the others is the Self. Jung called it "this central archetype", a complexio oppositorum par excellence, and said his leanings ran "towards the very reverse of dualism."205 His Self and the Self of the Śaiva sources stand in one place. Śiva–Śakti is the Self as awareness together with its power, and the Trika names its unspeakability Anuttara, the unsurpassable, beyond which nothing further can be said or sought.206 When we come to clarify the word consciousness, it is this Self that the word has been trying to name.

Jung's psychology is non-dual at the root, and the mandala images in which the Self appears have, as he and Pauli both noted, an empty centre. Where the physicist found complementary descriptions held around an observer who cannot step outside them, the psychologist found opposites held around a centre that cannot be filled. Each found, in his own register, that the either/or of classical logic could not describe what was in front of him. Quantum paradox and psychic non-duality press toward one requirement: a logical and an epistemic form able to hold two determinations that exclude each other in any single act and are both needed for the whole. That logical form is the symbolon (0/1)/(1/0). Its two orientations, AND and OR under one slash, are complementarity written as a logic, and QL is its epistemic method.

Both men located the meeting point in number. In March 1953 Pauli proposed homo-usia, borrowing the theological word for sameness of substance, for the essential identity he suspected between the archetypal world and physis. Such an identity could only be expressed, he argued, through concepts neutral between psyche and physis, and mathematical concepts are such concepts. "It is this number archetype," he wrote, "that ultimately makes possible the application of mathematics in physics," and the same archetype connects with the psyche through trinity, quaternity and the mantic arts.207 He held homo-usia as a working hypothesis, and he kept mathematical objects distinct from the psychic experience of doing mathematics, so that the claim never collapses into saying that a theorem is a feeling. Jung answered that October. His dreams and his intuition had both pointed him to the natural numbers as the simplest and most elementary archetypes, because whole numbers, given the chance, amplify themselves at once into mythological statement; and they show the psychoid character in classical form, since "they are as much inside as they are outside."208 As numbers they are inside, and as quantities they are outside, which is why no one can settle whether they were devised or discovered. He concluded that the borderland between physics and psychology lies in the secret of number, and he quoted the old saying that man made mathematics while God made the whole numbers. In the same letter he asked for something neither man went on to produce: an inventory of the numbers' mathematical properties set beside their symbolic amplification. Four years later Pauli offered, as a conjecture he was careful to keep a conjecture, that "Physis and Psyche are probably two aspects of one and the same abstract fact," and suggested a mirror-image principle as the natural way to picture their relation.209

At the height of its claim, this essay formalises what the correspondence left open. QL is the inventory Jung asked for, begun where his letter could not begin, at zero. It counts positions whose mathematical properties and symbolic offices are derived together from one relation, 0/1, so that the inside and the outside of each number are given in a single construction. Its derivation is the essay's own; the letters give it its history and its stakes, and neither correspondent supplies or verifies it. It also names Pauli's homo-usia. What we call Code is the common essence that lets mathematics apply to physics and lets number carry meaning for the psyche. §4 follows the dreams and disagreements through which Jung and Pauli kept their question open. What they bequeathed to the whole essay is the thesis that number is where psyche and physis meet, and QL is the form in which we take it up.

Neural language models give that thesis a concrete starting point. In 2003 Yoshua Bengio and his colleagues published "A Neural Probabilistic Language Model".210 Their problem is the one every learner of a language faces: almost every sentence one meets is new, so memorising sequences one at a time leaves nearly everything outside one's knowledge. A neural network is an arrangement of numerical transformations whose connection strengths are adjusted by training on examples, and a language model is such a network trained to predict linguistic sequences. Their answer was to let the network learn, together with the probabilities of word sequences, a position for every word in a mathematical space.
